## Prison of Zagyg

The prison of Zagyg was a minor artifact created by the Oerthian wizard Zagyg Yragerne that could imprison almost any creature. It was widely believed that there were only five copies of the artifact in existence.

Description
All copies of the prison of Zagyg were nearly identical, having the appearance of a small, well-made brass birdcage, studded with gems and sporting a padlock that had the appearance of diamond. They weighed no more than .

Powers
These artifacts were only usable by wizards and other arcane spellcasters.

Both the interior and exterior of a prison of Zagyg were constantly under the effect of permanent anti-magic and non-detection spells. The former effect caused any supernatural abilities, spell-like abilities, spells, and other magic possessed by a prisoner to not function within the cage. The latter effect made these cages and their prisoners impossible to locate by magical means, including by divinations of all sorts. If detect magic was ever cast upon a cage, the spell had only a 50% chance of detecting its magic, which was a strong abjuration dweomer. Otherwise, detect magic would either not discern what class of magic it was or give a false reading.

Additionally, any creature imprisoned within the confines of one of these cages was magically sustained, no longer requiring food or water.

Prisons of Zagyg were nearly impervious to damage, harm, or otherwise being affected by all manner of physical force or magic. However, each copy of the prison of Zagyg had its own weakness that could be exploited to cause its destruction. These methods were unknown, but guaranteed to be dangerous. If somehow one of these cages were destroyed, the creature imprisoned within them would be returned to their normal size. Alternatively, the spells alter reality or wish could be used to open a prison of Zagyg.

Operating a prison of Zagyg required attunement and knowledge of its four command words that activates it, though with tremendous expertise in the arcane a spellcaster could bypass the need for them. A prison of Zagyg would function only for those attuned to it, even if the appropriate command words were spoken. They could only be attuned to one creature at a time and upon changing ownership the previous owner was immediately made aware.

Each cage had own particular set of command words, separated into: activation, trigger or target, command spell or imprison, and freedom.

Action This command word had two effects. The first was to attune itself to whoever spoke it. The second effect spoking it had was activating its padlock, sealing the door to the cage shut as if wizard lock had been cast upon it.
Target By speaking this command word the possessor targeted a creature for imprisonment within the prison of Zagyg. According to some, this could only be done on creatures that were within  of the cage, while others claimed it had no limiting range.
Imprison Otherwise known as command spell, for some sources told of it being a distinct spell or incantation that had to be spoken, this command word teleported the target creature to the interior of the prison of Zagyg and shrunk their body to  in size. Those who claimed it to be an incantation said that the possessor had to include either the personal name of the creature being targeted or a detailed recitation of their history.
Freedom By speaking this command word the possessor caused the imprisoned creature to be teleported out of the prison of Zagyg, instantly restoring their former size and abilities. The possessor had control over where the the prisoner would be teleported to within  of the cage.

Creation
The methods and materials needed to create a prison of Zagyg were unknown, lost with their creator, though some of Zagyg's tomes contained partial instructions on how to go about creating one.

History
In the Oerthian year of 318 CY, Zagyg led the Company of Seven on an expedition to find the lost ruins of Veralos. Within its depths the wizard discovered a prototype magical cage that would serve as the basis for his prison of Zagyg.

Notable Owners
 Emelyn the Gray, a powerful archmage in the Bloodstone Lands of northeast Faerûn, possessed a prison of Zagyg in 1359 DR.
 Iggwilv, a powerful archmage from Oerth who had studied under Zagyg, stole one of his prisons and stored it along with other treasures inside the Lost Caverns of Tsojcanth. She had its command words written down in the Demonomicon of Iggwilv. These and other powerful artifacts fell out of her possession during her captivity in Azzagrat.
 The government of Ravens Bluff held a prison of Zagyg within their vaults circa 1353 DR. This copy was stolen by the wizard Panese the Excitable, who hid it beneath  of water in the bottom of a well within his basement, with a serpentine owl stored inside it. His theft landed the wizard a ten year prison sentence. He was offered the chance to commute his sentence to five years if he revealed where he hid the prison of Zagyg, but the wizard decided he would remain tight-lipped until he had served five years of his sentence.
 A will-o'-wisp in the caverns of Deepearth was in possession of one of these in 1358 DR.

Appendix
Background
The prison of Zagyg first appeared in the adventure module The Lost Caverns of Tsojcanth (1982). When that adventure was updated for 3 edition in the pages of Dungeon #151 (October 2007), the prison of Zagyg returned with its effect carried over almost verbatim. When the adventure was updated for 5 edition in the pages of Quests from the Infinite Staircase, the item was absent. In the prison of Zagyg's place the adventure featured a nameless golden birdcage that functioned similarly to an iron flask.

## Protection from dragon breath

Protection from dragon breath was a spell often embedded in enchanted scrolls for protecting against dragon breath.While FR6 introduced the spell, it is never mentioned again, and the spell is excluded even from the Spell Compendium, which explains that 2 edition makes it inappropriate to have such spells available. Furthermore, 2 edition updates 1 edition's scroll of protection against breath weapons – dragon to the scroll of protection against dragon breath, which mimics these functions. The duration remains consistent between the spell and both scrolls; it is assumed in this article that the spell is intended to mirror the effect of the scroll.

Effects
The spell protected one creature from the breath of any dragon, no matter the variety of the dragon or the breath, for anywhere from six to a dozen minutes.

Components
The Red Wizards knew a version of the spell that could be cast as any other. Its material component was a gold coin, destroyed by the casting. It also required verbal and somatic components. The spell, however, was mostly employed in the form of scrolls that would activate upon reading them aloud.

History
The spell was known to the Red Wizards of Thay, but had been abandoned by . Its use was far more common in the form of scrolls of protection by that time.

## Promise of wealth

Promise of wealth was a divination spell from the 6 circle of magic granted to the warlocks who had their pacts forged with Mammon, the King of Greed.

Effects
When the spell was cast, it infected a creature of the caster's choice with infectious greed and another creature with an aura that promised wealth. This granted the greedy creatures a vicious advantage in battle, dramatically increasing the chances of them landing attacks against the wealthy individuals.

The spell did not get empowered if the caster was a more powerful warlock. Its targets had to be visible to the caster and be within  of them. Lastly, the spell lasted for 12 seconds, and the caster had to maintain concentration.

History
Circa the, the promise of wealth spell was known to Helsik, the diabolist of Baldur's Gate, the Latchkeeper of the Nine Hells, the mistress of Devil's Fee shop in the Lower City.

## Prophia Copperfire

Prophia Copperfire was a renowned half-elven bard and traveler who crossed Toril and the planes long before the late 13 century DR.

History
Long before the, Prophia Copperfire owned the unique enchanted instrument called The Merry Shorthorn. She carried the instrument with her across Faerûn, and some even claimed the flute accompanied its mistress to the elemental planes, Elysium, and even Baator. She was known to have traveled across the Spine of the World and reached the distant nation of Wa. She entertained pashas of Calimshan, softened hearts of angry holy slayers in Zakhara, and was sung along by boisterous sailors of the Sea of Fallen Stars. Subsequently, The Merry Shorthorn was lost somewhere in the hot sands of Anauroch and Prophia's fate after that remained unknown.

## Praung

Praung was a powerful and ancient ornate enchanted short sword that belonged to Pelendralaar, a red dracolich in league with the Cult of the Dragon in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Praung possessed a powerful +7 enchantment that greatly increased the blade's damage output and guided the wielder's hand toward the target. The weapon also boosted the wielder's physical strength, slightly increased their reflexes, but greatly decreased their willpower. The weapon's true power, however, was its power to cast the resurrection spell. It held three charges of the said spell.

History
In the, the cultists who were led by Kya Mordrayn made way into the ruins of the ancient elven city of Myth Drannor to take control of a pool of radiance in the cavern cellars of Castle Cormanthor. Pelendralaar was to be submerged in the magical pool to greatly empower the undead creature; however, both the dracolich and Kya Mordrayn were defeated by the Veiled Ones. The Praung shortsword was recovered from Pelendralaar's corpse.

## Professor orb

A professor orb was a sentient magical orb imbued with academic knowledge.

Description
A professor orb was a smooth sphere of smoky gray quartz, approximately the size of a grapefruit, and weighing . Pinpricks of silver light shimmered from deep inside, indicating the magical intelligence within.

Powers
A professor orb could speak and read and could understand four languages, and perceived its environment through magical means.

It was able to cast mage hand at will, but only to move itself.

With scholarly personalities, professor orbs provided information and insight on its fields of expertise. Each had four different narrow areas of academic knowledge.

Known Professor Orbs

 Professor Skant, a lawful good orb owned by Vellynne Harpell and stolen by Nass Lantomir, expert in Netherese history, vampire lore, the tarrasque, and Elverquisst rituals.
 Professor Bring, a lawful neutral orb found in Dweomercore in Undermountain, expert in Undermountain history, identifying gemstones, xorn, and dwarven grooming habits.

## Professor Skant

Professor Skant was a professor orb, a sentient magical orb imbued with specialized academic knowledge.

Description
Professor Skant was a typical professor orb: a smooth sphere of smoky gray quartz weighing  and approximately the size of a grapefruit. Inside, pinpricks of silver light gleamed faintly, betraying the arcane presence inside.

Personality
The orb exhibited the personality of a scholar—learned, talkative, and prone to condescension. It regarded all humanoids as intellectually inferior. Professor Skant's personality was lawful good.

Powers
As a professor orb, it was capable of speech and could see and hear out to a distance of . Professor Skant communicated in Common, Draconic, Elvish, and Loross.

Professor Skant was an expert in four specialized academic fields: Netherese history; vampires and their traits; the tarrasque; and the rituals involving in producing, bottling, and consuming Elverquisst, a rare elven liquor.

Additionally, it could cast the mage hand cantrip as it willed, though it used this ability solely to maneuver itself.

History
It eventually came into the possession of the Harpells as a family heirloom and was used by Vellynne Harpell, a necromancer of the Arcane Brotherhood. During her visit to Icewind Dale in the, Professor Skant was stolen by Nass Lantomir, a rival member of the Brotherhood, who then perished on the isle of Solstice. The orb was later recovered by adventurers and may have been returned to Vellynne.  During subsequent events, Professor Skant served as a vital source of lore, particularly about the ancient city of Ythryn and Netherese civilization.

Notable Owners
 Vellynne Harpell, necromancer of the Arcane Brotherhood
 Nass Lantomir, former Arcane Brotherhood member

## Primordial naga

Primordial nagas, also known as fire nagas, were a form of mortal naga said to be spawned from the Elemental Chaos.

Description
They had five heads, which were triangular and flat with grotesque faces that looked vaguely like those of older humans with coarse and wicked features. They had forked tongues and yellow eyes, and the impression of eyebrows was given by a hard ridge over their bulbous noses. These features were lit with fire that burned even when they were wet.

Personality
These nagas were aggressive, preferring to fight than parlay in most situations, and the five heads even quarreled amongst each other. They delighted in terrorizing their victims, and found it mesmerizing to watch the fires of whatever they set alight. Unlike many other forms of naga, they were not interested in studying or making plans, and instead relied on their great strength.

Combat
Their preferred method of attack was to spit fire at their foes, which they could do from a range of up to . This was combined with other elemental attacks, such as slamming their enemies with wind or exhaling their freezing breath. Only when such powers were exhausted did they resort to flailing at their foes with their five heads.

Coming within  of a primordial naga exposed one to a cascade of fire and lightning. The nagas themselves were unaffected by this, and were resistant to several types of elemental magics. Their necks were thinnest and most vulnerable just beneath their chins.

Society
Primordial nagas stood apart from most other nagas, and it was said that their ancestor split from the ancestors of other nagas in mythic history. They were often encountered as guards of portals to the Elemental Chaos or as the protectors of the secrets of powerful primordial entities.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, fire nagas haunted Kork Bay at the narrowest point of the Strait of the Leviathan between the Moonshae Isles of Moray and Gwynneth. Very few survived their attacks, leading to rumors that approaching Moray by this Bay was cursed. One survivor of such attacks was the wereboar, Poke, who lost her boat and crew while trying to leave Moray. Another survivor, Captain Blau, would go on to recount in horror having seen the heads of fire nagas eating the survivors of a flaming shipwreck, and pulling sailors apart between their quarrelling heads. He would drunkenly tell this tale to Captain Lukas, who would go on to lose his ship, the Sphinx, to the same monsters.

## Protection from liquids

Protection from liquids was an abjuration spell designed to allow wizards to create a liquid-proof barrier around small object, particularly spellbooks, as its creator Spielbuk Spayle (a pseudonym) had lost his first ever spellbook to water damage inflicted by a dunk into a stream.

Effects
This spell could only be cast upon objects that were smaller than the spellcaster. It could not be cast upon a living creature. The magical barrier it created would protect from all manner of liquids. This included corrosive liquids like acid, hazardous blood like that of a greater medusa, and breath weapons of a liquid nature, like that of a black dragon.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, casting this spell required a material component in the form of a handful of dust, which was to be sprinkled over the desired target.

## Priam

Priam was an officer in the Red Plumes mercenary army of Hillsfar in the early-to-mid 14 century DR.

Description
By 1357 DR, Priam was a middle-aged man of forty-one winters. Sporting a tanned complexion and a trimmed, curly black beard, his armaments were standard for an officer.

Relationships
The only known family member of Priam was his uncle, the High Priest of Tyr in Melvaunt's temple.

History
In the, during the abduction of the Moonsea cities by Bane, Priam gathered the remaining survivors of Melvaunt into a makeshift army. With the fortunate aid of the Heroes of Phlan, they liberated Melvaunt's temple of Tyr. It was not known if he survived the attempt on his life.

## Primal mahuizzochimalli

Primal mahuizzochimalli and their more powerful variants, exalted primal mahuizzochimalli, were a variety of artifact shields created to be used by fighter adventurers in the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR.

Description
These primal mahuizzochimalli were testaments to their owners' strength and the skills of the master weapon-crafters of Chult. These items were infused with the savage energies of the powerful primal jungle beasts, and once equipped by adventurers, they filled them with the rage and strength of the ancestors. Primal mahuizzochimalli were created to be paired with primal macuahuitls, which were enchanted swords.

Powers
A primal mahuizzochimalli could be enchanted to hold various unique magics for each owner, in addition to boosting the damage inflicted by the owner in combat and increasing their ability to deflect enemy blows. When a primal mahuizzochimalli was wielded with a primal macuahuitl, both items boosted each other's magical powers, increasing the wielder's vitality, damage output, and boosting their ability to heal, magically and via mundane means.

History
Primal mahuizzochimalli were created in the late 15 century DR, and their design suggested the items' origins to be tied to the ancient Lost City of Omu.

Appendix
Background
A mahuizzochimalli was a type of a ceremonial Chīmalli or "Aztec shield". The Nahuatl word roughly translates to "splendid shield".

Appearances

References
